Indifference to Achievement: Insensitivity or indifference to personal achievements or success.\n  - Significant Weight Loss: Weight loss of more than 5% in a month without dieting.\n  - Weight Gain: Weight gain of more than 5% in a month.\n  - Decrease in Appetite: Decrease in appetite occurring nearly every day.\n  - Increase in Appetite: Increase in appetite occurring nearly every day.\n  - Recurrent Thoughts of Death: Passive wish to die (e.g., 'I wish I wouldn't wake up') without active intent to kill oneself.\n  - Suicidal Ideation: Recurring thoughts of 'I want to die' or 'I want to kill myself' without a specific plan.\n  - Suicidal Impulse: Strong, sudden urges to jump from a height or run into traffic without a concrete plan.\n  - This pattern is consistent with Bipolar II Disorder, which is characterized by at least one major depressive episode and at least one hypomanic episode. The fact that the patient's depressive symptoms are significantly less severe or absent during their periods of elevated mood also aligns with this diagnosis. However, when I'm in those high-energy phases, all of those depressive feelings completely disappear and are replaced by overflowing energy and an elevated mood.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how your thoughts, speech, and behavior change during these high-energy periods, and do you engage in any impulsive or reckless behaviors that you might not normally do when you're feeling stable?\n\nPatient: During those high-energy periods, my thoughts spin incredibly fast and flood my mind so much that I just can't seem to control or slow them down. I also become extremely hyperactive, starting multiple projects at once and constantly needing to stay in motion. I'm not really sure about engaging in any impulsive or reckless behaviors, though.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any breaks from reality during your high-energy periods, such as hearing or seeing things that aren't there, or having beliefs that seem unrealistic or detached from reality?\n\nPatient: No, I've never experienced anything like that. Even when my energy was at its highest, I never saw or heard things that weren't there, and I didn't have any unrealistic beliefs."
  }
]

So, those high phases actually persist for about twice as long as my low periods.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience significant depressive symptoms, such as loss of interest in activities, changes in appetite or sleep, or feelings of worthlessness, during your depressive episodes, and are these symptoms absent or significantly less severe during your periods of elevated mood?\n\nPatient: Yes, during my low periods, I completely lose interest in things I used to enjoy and my appetite changes drastically. However, when I'm in those high-energy phases, all of those depressive feelings completely disappear and are replaced by overflowing energy and an elevated mood.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how your thoughts, speech, and behavior change during these high-energy periods, and do you engage in any impulsive or reckless behaviors that you might not normally do when you're feeling stable?\n\nPatient: During those high-energy periods, my thoughts spin incredibly fast and flood my mind so much that I just can't seem to control or slow them down. I also become extremely hyperactive, starting multiple projects at once and constantly needing to stay in motion. I'm not really sure about engaging in any impulsive or reckless behaviors, though.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any breaks from reality during your high-energy periods, such as hearing or seeing things that aren't there, or having beliefs that seem unrealistic or detached from reality?\n\nPatient: No, I've never experienced anything like that. Even when my energy was at its highest, I never saw or heard things that weren't there, and I didn't have any unrealistic beliefs.\n\nDoctor: Can you confirm whether your high-energy periods have ever met the full criteria for a hypomanic episode, meaning they lasted at least four consecutive days and were associated with at least three specific symptoms, such as inflated self-esteem, decreased need for sleep, or excessive involvement in pleasurable activities?"
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes, those high-energy periods definitely lasted much longer than four days, usually persisting for about six months. I'm not really sure about having inflated self-esteem or a decreased need for sleep, though. But I definitely struggled with other symptoms during those times, like the constant restlessness and racing thoughts.

Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others. Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.
